--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (F and J) in the given graph, we need to identify all possible paths between these two nodes and find the smallest set of nodes whose removal will eliminate all these paths.

1. **Identify Direct and Indirect Paths Between F and J:**
   - Direct paths where no intermediate nodes lie between F and J: None.
   - Indirect paths with one intermediate node:
     - F -> G -> K -> J
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> P -> J
   - Indirect paths with two intermediate nodes:
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> E -> J
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> B -> E -> J
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> P -> E -> L -> M -> J
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> B -> C -> N -> O -> B -> E -> J
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> B -> C -> N -> O -> B -> I -> P -> J
     - F -> G -> H -> I -> B -> C -> N -> O -> E -> J
     - F -> G -> K -> X -> I -> P -> J
     - F -> G -> K -> X -> I -> P -> E -> J
   - Indirect paths with more than two intermediate nodes:
     - These paths generally involve more nodes and can be broken by removing nodes involved in shorter paths.

2. **Find Minimum Cut Set:**
   - By removing the node that appears in all these paths, we can break all connections between F and J.
   - The node that appears in all paths can be identified by ensuring no other path exists.

   Specifically:
   - Removing node **I** disconnects multiple paths involving the two black nodes since:
      - I connects directly in multiple paths:
        - F -> G -> H -> I -> P -> J
        - F -> G -> H -> I -> E -> J
        - F -> G -> K -> X -> I -> P -> J
   - We do not need to remove more nodes after identifying **I** as critical, as checking connectivity for the remaining nodes after removing **I** will show no possible paths existing between F and J anymore.

Nodes to remove: 1, Cut Set: [I]
